\textstyle{E\otimes_{\proj}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\otimes_{\alpha}Z_{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\otimes_{\alpha/\penalty}F}

together with
[
Pis03
, Prop. 2.4.1]
it follows that
\alpha/\penalty\leq\proj
.

Similarly, from the inequality
\min\leq\alpha
and the diagram

\textstyle{E\otimes_{\alpha}Z_{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\otimes_{\alpha/\penalty}F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\otimes_{\min}Z_{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\otimes_{\min}F}

it follows that
\min\leq\alpha/\penalty
.
Therefore,
\alpha/\penalty
is a reasonable operator space cross-norm.

Let us now show that that
\alpha/\penalty
has the complete metric mapping property.
Let
S\in\CB(E_{1},E_{2})
and
T\in\CB(F_{1},F_{2})
and consider complete quotients
Z_{j}\twoheadrightarrow F_{j}
with the spaces
Z_{j}
being completely projective.
Therefore, given
\varepsilon>0
there exists a completely bounded map
\widehat{T}:Z_{1}\to Z_{2}
such that
\|\widehat{T}\|_{\cb}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\left\|T\right\|_{\cb}
and such that the diagram

\textstyle{Z_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{T}}
\textstyle{Z_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{F_{2}}

commutes.
After tensorizing, we obtain

\textstyle{E_{1}\otimes_{\alpha}Z_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S\otimes\widehat{T}}
\textstyle{E_{2}\otimes_{\alpha}Z_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{1}\otimes_{\alpha}F_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S\otimes T}
\textstyle{E_{2}\otimes_{\alpha}F_{2}}

from where we clearly see, appealing to the complete metric mapping property of
\alpha
, that

\left\|S\otimes T:E_{1}\otimes_{\alpha/\penalty}F_{1}\to E_{2}\otimes_{\alpha/\penalty}F_{2}\right\|_{\cb}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\left\|S\right\|_{\cb}\left\|T\right\|_{\cb}.

Letting
\varepsilon\to 0
yields the complete metric mapping property of
\alpha/\penalty
.

Now, let
G\in\OBAN
and suppose that
q:F\twoheadrightarrow G
is a complete quotient.
If
q_{F}:Z_{F}\twoheadrightarrow F
is the standard complete quotient, observe that the composition

q\circ q_{F}:Z_{F}\twoheadrightarrow G
is also a complete quotient.
For
z\in M_{n}(E\otimes G)
, by what was shown at the beginning of the proof we have

\displaystyle\alpha/\penalty_{n}(z;E,G)

\displaystyle=\inf\big\{\alpha_{n}(w;E,Z_{F})\,:\,(q\circ q_{F})_{n}w=z\big\}

\displaystyle=\inf\big\{\alpha_{n}(w;E,Z_{F})\,:\,(q_{F})_{n}w=u,q_{n}u=z\big\}

\displaystyle=\inf\big\{\inf\{\alpha_{n}(w;E,Z_{F})\,:\,(q_{F})_{n}w=u\}\,:\,q_{n}u=z\big\}

\displaystyle=\inf\big\{\alpha/\penalty_{n}(u;E,F)\,:\,q_{n}u=z\big\}.

That is,
id_{E}\otimes q:E\otimes_{\alpha/\penalty}F\to E\otimes_{\alpha/\penalty}G
is a complete quotient and therefore
\alpha/\penalty
is completely right-projective in
\ONORM\times\OBAN
.

According to Lemma
10.11

(a)
we know that the norm
\alpha/\penalty
is finitely-generated from the right in
\ONORM\times\OBAN
. Thus, by Lemma
10.11

(b)
and
(c)
this norm can be extended to
\ONORM\times\ONORM
and this extension is completely right-projective on
\ONORM
.
Moreover, by definition
\alpha\leq\alpha/\penalty
on
\ONORM\times\OFIN
, which implies that

\alpha\leq\alpha^{\rightarrow}\leq(\alpha/\penalty)^{\rightarrow}=\alpha/\penalty.

Finally, let
\beta
be any completely right-projective norm that dominates
\alpha
. Then,
\alpha/\penalty\leq\beta
in
\ONORM\times\OFIN
by definition. Remark
10.12
says that
\beta=\beta^{\rightarrow}
on
\ONORM
then, by Lemma
10.11

(a)
, we have

\alpha/\penalty=(\alpha/\penalty)^{\rightarrow}\leq\beta^{\rightarrow}=\beta.

∎

Lemma 10.14
.

If
\alpha
be a finitely-generated o.s. tensor norm in
\ONORM
, then
\alpha/
is also finitely-generated.

Proof.

Let
z\in M_{n}(E\otimes F)
. By Lemma
10.11
we know that
\alpha/
is finitely-generated on the right. Then, for a given
\varepsilon>0
there is a subspace
N\in\OFIN(F)
satisfying

(\alpha/)_{n}(z;E,N)\leq(1+\varepsilon)(\alpha/)_{n}(z;E,F).

Since
N
is finite dimensional it is complete. Hence, there is a completely projective Banach operator space
Z_{N}
such that

id_{E}\otimes q_{N}:E\otimes_{\alpha}Z_{N}\twoheadrightarrow E\otimes_{\alpha/}N

is a complete quotient.
Then there is an element
w\in M_{n}(E\otimes Z_{N})
with
(id_{E}\otimes q_{N})_{n}(w)=z

and

\alpha_{n}(w;E,Z_{N})\leq(1+\varepsilon)(\alpha/)_{n}(z;E,N).

Now, due to
\alpha
being finitely-generated there exists
M\in\OFIN(E)
such that

\alpha_{n}(w;M,Z_{N})\leq(1+\varepsilon)\alpha_{n}(w;E,Z_{N}).

Note that
id_{M}\otimes q_{N}:E\otimes_{\alpha}Z_{N}\to E\otimes_{\alpha/}N
has norm one and
(id_{M}\otimes q_{N})_{n}(w)=z
, therefore

(\alpha/)_{n}(z;M,N)\leq\alpha_{n}(w;M,Z_{N})\leq(1+\varepsilon)^{3}\alpha_{n}(z;E,F).

∎

The following lemma will be useful for our purposes.

Lemma 10.15
.

Let
\alpha
be an o.s. tensor norm finitely-generated from the right then
\backslash\alpha
is finitely-generated.

Proof.

Let
E,F\in\ONORM
and
z\in M_{n}(E\otimes F)
. By the left version of Remark
10.12
we know that
\backslash\alpha
is finitely-generated from the left. Then, given
\varepsilon>0
there exists
M\in\OFIN(E)
such that
z\in M_{n}(M\otimes F)
and

(\backslash\alpha)_{n}(z;M,F)\leq(1+\varepsilon)(\backslash\alpha)_{n}(z;E,F).

Applying the left version of Theorem
10.13
(and using that
M\in\OFIN\subset\OBAN
) we have that there exists
u\in M_{n}(Z_{M}\otimes F)
satisfying
(q_{M}\otimes id_{F})_{n}(u)=z
and

\alpha_{n}(u;Z_{M},F)\leq(1+\varepsilon)(\backslash\alpha)_{n}(z;M,F).

Since
\alpha
is finitely-generated from the right, there is a space
N\in\OFIN(F)
such that
u\in M_{n}(Z_{M}\otimes N)
and

\alpha_{n}(u;Z_{M},N)\leq(1+\varepsilon)\alpha_{n}(u;Z_{M},F).

The mapping

q_{M}\otimes id_{N}:Z_{M}\otimes_{\alpha}N\twoheadrightarrow M\otimes_{\backslash\alpha}N

satisfies
(q_{M}\otimes id_{N})_{n}(u)=z
. This implies that
z\in M_{n}(M\otimes N)
and

(\backslash\alpha)_{n}(z;M,N)\leq\alpha_{n}(u;Z_{M},N),

which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 10.16
.

All the previous results about completely right-injective and completely right-projective o.s. tensor norms have left versions with analogous proofs. Specifically, there are valid left statements of Remark
10.2
, Proposition
10.3
, Corollary
10.4
, Theorem
10.5
, Lemma
10.6
. Also, after defining the left-finite hull of an o.s. tensor norm, there are left adaptations of Lemma
10.11
, Remark
10.12
, Theorem
10.13
, Lemma
10.14
and Lemma
10.15
.

Definition 10.17
.

The o.s. tensor norm
\alpha/\penalty
given in Theorem
10.13
is called the
completely right-projective hull
of
\alpha
.
One can define analogously the
completely left-projective hull
of
\alpha
, denoted
\backslash\alpha
, and the
completely projective hull

\backslash\alpha/\penalty:=(\backslash\alpha)/\penalty=\backslash(\alpha/\penalty).

Note that there is no ambiguity in the previous equality since
\backslash(\alpha/\penalty)
and
(\backslash\alpha)/\penalty
are equal. Indeed, let
E,F\in OBAN
and
Z_{E}
and
Z_{F}
completely projective operator spaces such that

q_{E}:Z_{E}\twoheadrightarrow E\quad\textrm{and}\quad q_{F}:Z_{E}\twoheadrightarrow F

are complete quotients then we known that both norms
(\backslash\alpha)/\penalty
and
\backslash(\alpha/\penalty)
coincide on
E\otimes F
and can be computed through the complete quotient

q_{E}\otimes q_{F}:Z_{E}\otimes_{\alpha}Z_{F}\twoheadrightarrow E\otimes_{\backslash\alpha/\penalty}F.

Using Remark
10.12
we know that
\alpha/\penalty
is finitely-generated from the right and therefore, by Lemma
10.15
, the o.s. tensor norm
\backslash(\alpha/\penalty)
is finitely-generated. The same argument can be used to prove that
(\backslash\alpha)/\penalty
is finitely-generated. Since both norms
\backslash(\alpha/\penalty)
and
(\backslash\alpha)/\penalty
coincide on
\OBAN
and they are finitely-generated, they must be equal on
\ONORM
.

The operator space
S_{1}(H)
is not completely projective
[
Ble92a
]
, but with respect to completely right- (or left-) projective hulls of a finitely generated o.s. tensor norm it behaves as if it was. The reason behind this is the fact that

S_{1}(H)
is an
\mathscr{OS}_{1,1+}
space (see Section
2.7
):
each finite-dimensional subspace of
S_{1}(H)
is contained in a larger finite-dimensional subspace which is “almost a copy” of
S_{1}^{k}
for some
k
:

Proposition 10.18
.

[
ER94
, Prop. 5.3]

Given
M\in\OFIN(S_{1}(H))
and
\varepsilon>0
there exist a subspace
\widetilde{M}\in\OFIN(S_{1}(H))
, a number
k\in\mathbb{N}
and a complete isomorphism
T:\widetilde{M}\to S_{1}^{k}
such that
\widetilde{M}\supset M
,
\|T\|_{cb}\leq 1+\varepsilon
and
\|T^{-1}\|_{cb}\leq 1+\varepsilon
.

Now we can state and prove the result mentioned above.

Proposition 10.19
.

Let
\alpha
be a finitely-generated o.s. tensor norm and let
E\in\ONORM
. Then,

E\otimes_{\alpha}S_{1}(H)=E\otimes_{\alpha/}S_{1}(H)\quad\textrm{ and }\quad S_{1}(H)\otimes_{\alpha}E=S_{1}(H)\otimes_{\backslash\alpha}E.

Proof.

Since
\alpha\leq\alpha/
in order to prove the first identity (the other is obtained by transposition) we have just to show for any
z\in M_{n}(E\otimes S_{1}(H))
that
(\alpha/)_{n}(z;E,S_{1}(H))\leq\alpha_{n}(z;E,S_{1}(H))
. Given
\varepsilon>0
there is
M\in\OFIN(S_{1}(H))
satisfying
z\in M_{n}(E\otimes M)
and

(10.1)

\alpha_{n}(z;E,M)\leq(1+\varepsilon)\alpha_{n}(z;E,S_{1}(H)).

By Proposition
10.18
there is
\widetilde{M}\supset M
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
and a complete isomorphism
T:\widetilde{M}\to S_{1}^{k}
such that
\|T\|_{cb}\leq 1+\varepsilon
and
\|T^{-1}\|_{cb}\leq 1+\varepsilon
. Then

(10.2)

\alpha_{n}((id\otimes T)_{n}(z);E,S_{1}^{k})\leq(1+\varepsilon)\alpha_{n}(z;E,\widetilde{M})\leq(1+\varepsilon)\alpha_{n}(z;E,M).

Now, applying
T^{-1}
and using that
S_{1}^{k}
is completely projective we have

(10.3)

(\alpha/)_{n}(z;E,\widetilde{M})\leq(1+\varepsilon)\alpha_{n}((id\otimes T)_{n}(z);E,S_{1}^{k}).

Finally, since
(\alpha/)_{n}(z;E,S_{1}(H))\leq(\alpha/)_{n}(z;E,\widetilde{M})
compounding the identities (
10.1
), (
10.2
) and (
10.3
) we arrive to

(\alpha/)_{n}(z;E,S_{1}(H))\leq(1+\varepsilon)^{3}\alpha_{n}(z;E,S_{1}(H)),

which concludes the proof.
∎

As a consequence of the previous result we present the following operator space version of a classical Banach space identity.

Example 10.20
.

d_{\infty}=\min/
.

Indeed, since both norms are finitely-generated (by Proposition
3.5
and Lemma
10.15
) it is enough to prove that
M\otimes_{d_{\infty}}N=M\otimes_{\min/}N
for every
M,N\in\OFIN
.
Using that every separable operator space is completely isometric to a quotient of
S_{1}

[
Pis03
, Cor. 2.12.3]
and that both
d_{\infty}
and
\min/
are completely right-projective, we know that there is a complete quotient
q:S_{1}\twoheadrightarrow N
which produces two other complete quotients

id\otimes q:M\otimes_{d_{\infty}}S_{1}\twoheadrightarrow M\otimes_{d_{\infty}}N\quad\textrm{and}\quad id\otimes q:M\otimes_{\min/}S_{1}\twoheadrightarrow M\otimes_{\min/}N.

Hence, the result is obtained once we prove
M\otimes_{d_{\infty}}S_{1}=M\otimes_{\min/}S_{1}
for every
M\in\OFIN
. This is certainly true because
M\otimes_{d_{\infty}}S_{1}=M\otimes_{\min}S_{1}
(by
[
CD16
, Th. 3.7]
) and
M\otimes_{\min/}S_{1}=M\otimes_{\min}S_{1}
(by Proposition
10.19
).

Another consequence of Propositions
10.18
and
10.19
is the following statement that will be useful later. The proof is obtained proceeding as in Example
10.20
along with Proposition
10.19
.

Corollary 10.21
.

Let
E,F\in\ONORM
with
F
separable and let
\alpha
be a finitely-generated o.s. tensor norm. If
z\in M_{n}(E\otimes_{\alpha/}F)
and
\varepsilon>0
there exist a number
k
, a mapping
R:S_{1}^{k}\to F
and a matrix
u\in M_{n}(E\otimes_{\alpha}S_{1}^{k})
such that
(id\otimes R)_{n}(u)=z
,
\|R\|_{cb}\leq 1+\varepsilon
and

\alpha_{n}(u;E,S_{1}^{k})\leq(1+\varepsilon)(\alpha/)_{n}(z;E,F).

11.
Mapping ideals associated to completely injective/projective hulls and accessibility

In Corollary
10.4
we have shown that the dual of a completely right-projective (completely left-projective) o.s. tensor norm is completely right-injective (completely left-injective). In the Banach space setting the reciprocal is valid but in the operator space context this is no longer true (see Remark
12.5
below). Note that
all known proofs of this fact for Banach space tensor norms use elements of the theory mentioned in Subsection
2.9
.

Hence, in order to produce a result relating completely
injective/projective hulls with duality we have to appeal for an extra hypothesis of accessibility.

Even if previously we presented in detail results about right hulls and obtain by analogy the left statements, now,
to see the other side of the moon
, we chose to proceed first with left hulls and to derive by similarity the right versions.

Proposition 11.1
.

Let
\alpha
be a finitely-generated o.s. tensor norm.

(a)

If
\alpha
is left-accessible then
(\backslash\alpha^{\prime})^{\prime}=/\alpha
and
(/\alpha)^{\prime}=\backslash\alpha^{\prime}
.

(b)

If
\alpha
is right-accessible then
(\alpha^{\prime}/)^{\prime}=\alpha\backslash
and
(\alpha\backslash)^{\prime}=\alpha^{\prime}/
.

(c)

If
\alpha
is totally accessible then
(\backslash\alpha^{\prime}/)^{\prime}=/\alpha\backslash
and
(/\alpha\backslash)^{\prime}=\backslash\alpha^{\prime}/
.

Proof.

We will only prove
(a)
since
(b)
and
(c)
follow analogously. To see the first identity, it suffices to check it in
\OFIN
since both norms are finitely-generated. Indeed, the norm
(\backslash\alpha^{\prime})^{\prime}
is finitely-generated since it is a dual norm and
/\alpha
is so by the left version of Lemma
10.6
.

Let
M
and
N
be finite dimensional operator spaces. We have to see that

(11.1)

\big(M\otimes_{\backslash\alpha^{\prime}}N^{\prime}\big)^{\prime}=M^{\prime}\otimes_{/\alpha}N.

To check (
11.1
) we consider the following diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 30.90318pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.90318pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-18.50005pt\raise-16.00891pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M^{\prime}\otimes_{/\alpha}N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.09244pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.32726pt\raise-29.01782pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.90318pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 54.90318pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
